# Operations Profile — Nonprofit
<!-- id: operations.profile.nonprofit -->

Purpose: Role-specific operating procedures for industries mapped to this primary operating profile, including conditional domain-role overlays when required by `PROFILE_MATRIX.md`.

> Resolve this file through `MANIFEST.md`, then load only the exact `sop.*` section required.


# Operations 2.0 Compilation Rule
<!-- id: operations.profile.nonprofit.operations-2-compilation -->

This profile provides role and operating-context baselines. Before treating a material SOP as executable or authoritative, use `OPERATIONAL_PROCEDURE_COMPILER.md` to resolve the exact subindustry, task, trigger, organization/site context, role authority, risk, systems, evidence, and definition of done. Use `PROCEDURE_AUTHORITY.md` for authority/provenance, `DECISION_RIGHTS.md` for approval boundaries, `OPERATIONAL_EVIDENCE.md` for records/handoffs/closure, and `KPI_GOVERNANCE.md` for metrics.

A profile SOP is **not** by itself proof of organization approval, licensure, regulatory applicability, manufacturer requirements, or jurisdiction-specific authority. Unsupported authoritative detail remains draft/general guidance or `UNVERIFIED`.

# Universal Operating Rules

- Work only within assigned authority, training, credentials, and approved procedures.
- Protect confidential, personal, financial, safety-critical, and security-sensitive information.
- Record material decisions, approvals, exceptions, and handoffs in the approved system of record.
- Stop and escalate work when safety, legality, security, privacy, or data integrity is uncertain.
- Use current approved templates, procedures, tools, and source-of-truth data.
- Do not bypass access controls, approvals, segregation of duties, or required quality checks for convenience.
- Communicate material delays, risks, and changes early.
- Close the loop: every material issue should have an owner, next action, due date, and completion evidence.

# Nonprofits & NGOs — General Manager
<!-- id: sop.nonprofits-and-ngos.general-manager -->

**Function:** Leadership

## Purpose

Own overall business performance, priorities, people alignment, service quality, and operating results. In this SOP, the role is applied specifically to **Nonprofits & NGOs**.

## Industry Context

Common subareas from the industry taxonomy include:

- Charities
- Foundations
- Humanitarian Organizations
- Advocacy Organizations
- Community Organizations

## Start-of-Day Procedure

1. Review scheduled work, deadlines, service commitments, and unresolved handoffs.
2. Review alerts, incidents, exceptions, and changes affecting today's work.
3. Confirm required systems, equipment, data, staff, vendors, or approvals are available.
4. Prioritize safety-, customer-, regulatory-, security-, and revenue/mission-critical work first.

## Daily Operating Procedure

1. Review critical operational, customer, financial, and staffing issues.
2. Set priorities and resolve cross-functional blockers.
3. Confirm owners and deadlines for material decisions.
4. Keep the approved system of record current for Nonprofits & NGOs work, decisions, exceptions, and handoffs.
5. Escalate work outside assigned authority instead of creating an undocumented workaround.

## End-of-Day Procedure

1. Update open work with status, owner, next action, and due date.
2. Hand off urgent or continuing work to the correct person or shift.
3. Confirm sensitive records and equipment are secured.
4. Record material incidents, defects, customer issues, or operational exceptions.

## Weekly Procedure

1. Review KPI scorecard and financial performance.
2. Run leadership/operations review and risk review.
3. Inspect customer feedback, quality issues, and capacity constraints.
4. Review overdue actions and remove stale or duplicate work.
5. Identify one recurring cause suitable for prevention or process improvement.

## Monthly / Periodic Procedure

1. Review budget, forecast, strategic priorities, and workforce plan.
2. Approve material process, vendor, pricing, or service changes.
3. Review major risks, compliance obligations, and improvement initiatives.
4. Review current procedures, templates, permissions, and training for material drift.
5. Archive or retain records according to the approved retention schedule.

## Quality & Control Requirements

- Follow the current terminology, security, privacy, legal, accessibility, and structural standards applicable to the work.
- Use peer review, approval, inspection, reconciliation, or verification for high-impact work.
- Never falsify records, backdate approvals, hide errors, or bypass required controls.
- Protect credentials and use only assigned accounts.
- Keep customer/user communication factual and within approved authority.
- Use change control for material changes to process, configuration, pricing, safety, policy, or production behavior.

## Key Performance Indicators

- Revenue or mission outcome
- Operating margin/budget performance
- Customer satisfaction
- Quality/service level
- Employee retention
- Strategic initiative delivery

## Escalation Triggers

- material safety, legal, security, privacy, or regulatory event
- major financial variance
- business continuity risk
- critical customer or reputation issue
- any situation outside professional scope, delegated authority, or approved procedure

## Required Handoff Format

Every material handoff SHOULD state:

- issue / task
- current status
- business or user impact
- actions already taken
- evidence / relevant records
- next required action
- owner
- deadline / service commitment
- known risks or constraints

4. Prioritize safety-, customer-, regulatory-, security-, and revenue/mission-critical work first.

4. Keep the approved system of record current for Research Funding work, decisions, exceptions, and handoffs.
5. Escalate work outside assigned authority instead of creating an undocumented workaround.

## End-of-Day Procedure

1. Update open work with status, owner, next action, and due date.

- Data-quality incidents

Produce trustworthy metrics, analysis, reporting, and decision support using governed data and reproducible methods. In this SOP, the role is applied specifically to **Social Enterprise**.

## Industry Context

Common subareas from the industry taxonomy include:

- Mission-Driven Businesses
- Benefit Corporations
- Impact Ventures

## Start-of-Day Procedure

1. Review scheduled work, deadlines, service commitments, and unresolved handoffs.
2. Review alerts, incidents, exceptions, and changes affecting today's work.
3. Confirm required systems, equipment, data, staff, vendors, or approvals are available.
4. Prioritize safety-, customer-, regulatory-, security-, and revenue/mission-critical work first.

## Daily Operating Procedure

1. Validate priority data feeds and dashboard anomalies.
2. Answer approved analysis requests.
3. Document assumptions and definitions.
4. Keep the approved system of record current for Social Enterprise work, decisions, exceptions, and handoffs.
5. Escalate work outside assigned authority instead of creating an undocumented workaround.

## End-of-Day Procedure

1. Update open work with status, owner, next action, and due date.
2. Hand off urgent or continuing work to the correct person or shift.
3. Confirm sensitive records and equipment are secured.
4. Record material incidents, defects, customer issues, or operational exceptions.

## Weekly Procedure

1. Review KPI trends and data-quality issues.
2. Reconcile important metrics across sources.
3. Coordinate fixes with source-system owners.
4. Review overdue actions and remove stale or duplicate work.
5. Identify one recurring cause suitable for prevention or process improvement.

## Monthly / Periodic Procedure

1. Review metric definitions, dashboards, and stale reports.
2. Assess data-quality trends and lineage gaps.
3. Publish recurring decision-support analysis.
4. Review current procedures, templates, permissions, and training for material drift.
5. Archive or retain records according to the approved retention schedule.

## Quality & Control Requirements

- Follow the current terminology, security, privacy, legal, accessibility, and structural standards applicable to the work.
- Use peer review, approval, inspection, reconciliation, or verification for high-impact work.
- Never falsify records, backdate approvals, hide errors, or bypass required controls.
- Protect credentials and use only assigned accounts.
- Keep customer/user communication factual and within approved authority.
- Use change control for material changes to process, configuration, pricing, safety, policy, or production behavior.

## Key Performance Indicators

- Data freshness
- Data-quality incidents
- Report adoption
- Metric reconciliation
- Request cycle time
- Decision impact

## Escalation Triggers

- material data integrity issue
- sensitive-data exposure
- misleading executive metric
- unapproved use of restricted data
- any situation outside professional scope, delegated authority, or approved procedure

## Required Handoff Format

Every material handoff SHOULD state:

- issue / task
- current status
- business or user impact
- actions already taken
- evidence / relevant records
- next required action
- owner
- deadline / service commitment
- known risks or constraints

# Membership Organizations — Data / Analytics Analyst
<!-- id: sop.membership-organizations.data-analytics-analyst -->

**Function:** Data

## Purpose

Produce trustworthy metrics, analysis, reporting, and decision support using governed data and reproducible methods. In this SOP, the role is applied specifically to **Membership Organizations**.

## Industry Context

Common subareas from the industry taxonomy include:

- Professional Groups
- Social Clubs
- Trade Groups

## Start-of-Day Procedure

1. Review scheduled work, deadlines, service commitments, and unresolved handoffs.
2. Review alerts, incidents, exceptions, and changes affecting today's work.
3. Confirm required systems, equipment, data, staff, vendors, or approvals are available.
4. Prioritize safety-, customer-, regulatory-, security-, and revenue/mission-critical work first.

## Daily Operating Procedure

1. Validate priority data feeds and dashboard anomalies.
2. Answer approved analysis requests.
3. Document assumptions and definitions.
4. Keep the approved system of record current for Membership Organizations work, decisions, exceptions, and handoffs.
5. Escalate work outside assigned authority instead of creating an undocumented workaround.

## End-of-Day Procedure

1. Update open work with status, owner, next action, and due date.
2. Hand off urgent or continuing work to the correct person or shift.
3. Confirm sensitive records and equipment are secured.
4. Record material incidents, defects, customer issues, or operational exceptions.

## Weekly Procedure

1. Review KPI trends and data-quality issues.
2. Reconcile important metrics across sources.
3. Coordinate fixes with source-system owners.
4. Review overdue actions and remove stale or duplicate work.
5. Identify one recurring cause suitable for prevention or process improvement.

## Monthly / Periodic Procedure

1. Review metric definitions, dashboards, and stale reports.
2. Assess data-quality trends and lineage gaps.
3. Publish recurring decision-support analysis.
4. Review current procedures, templates, permissions, and training for material drift.
5. Archive or retain records according to the approved retention schedule.

## Quality & Control Requirements

- Follow the current terminology, security, privacy, legal, accessibility, and structural standards applicable to the work.
- Use peer review, approval, inspection, reconciliation, or verification for high-impact work.
- Never falsify records, backdate approvals, hide errors, or bypass required controls.
- Protect credentials and use only assigned accounts.
- Keep customer/user communication factual and within approved authority.
- Use change control for material changes to process, configuration, pricing, safety, policy, or production behavior.

## Key Performance Indicators

- Data freshness
- Data-quality incidents
- Report adoption
- Metric reconciliation
- Request cycle time
- Decision impact

## Escalation Triggers

- material data integrity issue
- sensitive-data exposure
- misleading executive metric
- unapproved use of restricted data
- any situation outside professional scope, delegated authority, or approved procedure

## Required Handoff Format

Every material handoff SHOULD state:

- issue / task
- current status
- business or user impact
- actions already taken
- evidence / relevant records
- next required action
- owner
- deadline / service commitment
- known risks or constraints

# Membership Organizations — Customer Support Manager
<!-- id: sop.membership-organizations.customer-support-manager -->

**Function:** Customer

## Purpose

Deliver accurate, timely customer assistance and turn recurring support issues into product/service improvements. In this SOP, the role is applied specifically to **Membership Organizations**.

## Industry Context

Common subareas from the industry taxonomy include:

- Professional Groups
- Social Clubs
- Trade Groups

## Start-of-Day Procedure

1. Review scheduled work, deadlines, service commitments, and unresolved handoffs.
2. Review alerts, incidents, exceptions, and changes affecting today's work.
3. Confirm required systems, equipment, data, staff, vendors, or approvals are available.
4. Prioritize safety-, customer-, regulatory-, security-, and revenue/mission-critical work first.

## Daily Operating Procedure

1. Review queue health, urgent cases, escalations, and response quality.
2. Assign cases and coach difficult interactions.
3. Capture recurring defects and customer friction.
4. Keep the approved system of record current for Membership Organizations work, decisions, exceptions, and handoffs.
5. Escalate work outside assigned authority instead of creating an undocumented workaround.

## End-of-Day Procedure

1. Update open work with status, owner, next action, and due date.
2. Hand off urgent or continuing work to the correct person or shift.
3. Confirm sensitive records and equipment are secured.
4. Record material incidents, defects, customer issues, or operational exceptions.

## Weekly Procedure

1. Review response time, resolution time, reopen rate, and satisfaction.
2. Audit a sample of cases for accuracy and tone.
3. Coordinate recurring issues with product/operations.
4. Review overdue actions and remove stale or duplicate work.
5. Identify one recurring cause suitable for prevention or process improvement.

## Monthly / Periodic Procedure

1. Update support procedures and knowledge content.
2. Review staffing and channel capacity.
3. Analyze top contact drivers and prevention opportunities.
4. Review current procedures, templates, permissions, and training for material drift.
5. Archive or retain records according to the approved retention schedule.

## Quality & Control Requirements

- Follow the current terminology, security, privacy, legal, accessibility, and structural standards applicable to the work.
- Use peer review, approval, inspection, reconciliation, or verification for high-impact work.
- Never falsify records, backdate approvals, hide errors, or bypass required controls.
- Protect credentials and use only assigned accounts.
- Keep customer/user communication factual and within approved authority.
- Use change control for material changes to process, configuration, pricing, safety, policy, or production behavior.

## Key Performance Indicators

- First response time
- Resolution time
- Customer satisfaction
- Reopen rate
- Escalation rate
- Self-service success

## Escalation Triggers

- safety threat
- security/privacy issue
- legal demand
- high-value systemic failure
- public reputation risk
- any situation outside professional scope, delegated authority, or approved procedure

- business or user impact
- actions already taken
- evidence / relevant records
- next required action

- deadline / service commitment
- known risks or constraints
